  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Isabella, leader of Spain (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Spain
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Conquistador, Replacing Explorer (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Inquisition, Replacing Inquisitor (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Armada, Replacing Corvette (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Building: Bullring, Replacing Zoo (Requires technology: Scientific Theory)
        - Unique Improvement: Hacienda (Cannot be built adjacent to another Hacienda. Gains additional yields for each adjacent Resource:
          +3 Food from Bonus Resources
          +3 Production from Strategic Resources
          +3 Gold from Luxury Resources); Requires technology: Steel
        - Civilization Ability: +10 Gold and +4 Faith from gaining tiles, scaling with Era. Newly founded Cities adopt the Majority Religion of your Capital. May purchase Naval Units with Faith.
      - Leader: Isabella
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Satellites: Allow you to build the Apollo Program, a project necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: The Internet, Lasers
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Cult of Personality: +50% Tourism to civilizations fighting a common enemy. 50% of your highest Warscore counts as a Tourism Modifier with all Civilizations. Free Great Person of your choice.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Rationalism (New Branch):
        - Rationalism focuses on maximizing Growth and Science output.
        - Adopting Rationalism grants:
        - * +2 Production and +3 Science from all Strategic Resources.
        - * +5% Science in all Cities.
        - Each Rationalism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +2% Science.
        - Adopting all Policies in Rationalism grants:
        - * Unlocks building Bletchley Park.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +12 City Security in every City.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Scientists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 10
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 10
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 10
        - WarmongerHate: 4
        - NeutralBias: 5
        - FriendlyBias: 2
        - GuardedBias: 10
        - AfraidBias: 5
        - DiplomaticBalance: 2
        - Friendliness: 5
        - WorkWithWillingness: 10
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 3
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 7
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 7
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 6
        - MinorCivWarBias: 8
        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 1
        - Meanness: 10
        - Neediness: 8
        - Chattiness: 10
        - DeceptiveBias: 10
        - Rationale: Increase DeceptiveBias to 10 to maintain facade of friendship with Russia until we are ready to strike. Raise MinorCivHostileBias and MinorCivWarBias to be more aggressive towards enemy-aligned city-states, especially after losing M'banza-Kongo to The Netherlands.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Satellites
        - Rationale: Maintain Satellites as next research target. This completes our path to Apollo Program, enabling science victory pursuit as a backup while providing late-game strategic advantages. With peace secured with Songhai, we can focus on technological advancement without immediate military pressure. Satellites also unlocks critical components for space race and advanced military units, supporting our dual victory strategy.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Cult of Personality (Policy)
        - Rationale: Proceed with Cult of Personality as next policy adoption. The substantial tourism boost against civilizations fighting a common enemy will be highly effective when we declare war on Russia. The free Great Person (likely Great Scientist or Great General) provides immediate strategic value to accelerate research or bolster armies. This Autocracy tenet synergizes with our aggressive expansionist ideology and current diplomatic posture against Russia.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Ensure Brazil remains friendly and does not honor its defensive pact with Russia when we attack.
        - UpdatedTurn: 245
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Increase public relationship to match private, solidifying our defensive pact and ensuring The Netherlands remains a steadfast ally willing to join us in war against Russia.
        - UpdatedTurn: 246
        
        ### America
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Increase relations with America to 100/100 to solidify their neutrality and ensure they do not honor any potential defensive pacts with Russia when we declare war. America is a major power with strong city-state alliances, and keeping them friendly secures trade routes and reduces diplomatic risks.
        - UpdatedTurn: 256
        
        ### Korea
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Increase relations with Korea to 100/100 to reinforce our Declaration of Friendship and guarantee their continued neutrality during the upcoming war with Russia. Korea is a valuable trade partner and a friendly neighbor, and higher relations will prevent any shift in their allegiance.
        - UpdatedTurn: 256
        
        ### Russia
        - Public: 100
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Maintain public friendship (100) with Russia to keep defensive pacts inactive and trade routes flowing, while private hostility (-50) reflects strategic preparation for eventual conflict. Russia remains our top competitor with nuclear weapons and strong military; we must avoid triggering war prematurely while building our forces and isolating them diplomatically.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Maintain maximum hostility with Songhai to ensure no premature peace is considered. We must eventually eliminate their remaining city (Timbisha) to secure our position and remove them as a competitor. Their navy and air force remain formidable, and we need to keep pressure through sanctions and military posturing.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Maintain maximum hostility with The Shoshone to ensure no peace is considered. We must eliminate their last city (Hukandeka) to secure our position and remove their cultural/diplomatic threat. Their vassal status to Songhai makes them a secondary but necessary target after we deal with Songhai's main forces.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Increase UseNuke to 80 to ensure a more aggressive nuclear posture when we initiate war with Russia. Our military buildup is progressing well, with atomic bombs in production across multiple cities. Maintaining the Conquest grand strategy keeps our focus on capturing enemy capitals for a domination victory, while high UseNuke ensures we leverage our nuclear arsenal decisively against Russian cities and forces when the time comes.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 41

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 396
          - World Religion: Catholicism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.

              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 396
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 396
          - Spanish Sphere of Influence: Ife:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 396
          - Sanction: Songhai:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 396
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 396
          - Open Door: Bornu:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 396
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Songhai Sphere of Influence: Vienna:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: Songhai
          - Repeal: Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - Proposer: The Shoshone
          - Enact: Open Door: Veligrad:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - Proposer: Spain

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Isabella, leader of Spain (Opponent 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Spain
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Conquistador, Replacing Explorer (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Inquisition, Replacing Inquisitor (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Armada, Replacing Corvette (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Building: Bullring, Replacing Zoo (Requires technology: Scientific Theory)
        - Unique Improvement: Hacienda (Cannot be built adjacent to another Hacienda. Gains additional yields for each adjacent Resource:
          +3 Food from Bonus Resources
          +3 Production from Strategic Resources
          +3 Gold from Luxury Resources); Requires technology: Steel
        - Civilization Ability: +10 Gold and +4 Faith from gaining tiles, scaling with Era. Newly founded Cities adopt the Majority Religion of your Capital. May purchase Naval Units with Faith.
      - Leader: Isabella
      - OpponentID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Satellites: Allow you to build the Apollo Program, a project necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: The Internet, Lasers
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late world Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Cult of Personality: +50% Tourism to civilizations fighting a common enemy. 50% of your highest Warscore counts as a Tourism Modifier with all Civilizations. Free Great Person of your choice.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Rationalism (New Branch):
        - Rationalism focuses on maximizing Growth and Science output.
        - Adopting Rationalism grants:
        - * +2 Production and +3 Science from all Strategic Resources.
        - * +5% Science in all Cities.
        - Each Rationalism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +2% Science.
        - Adopting all Policies in Rationalism grants:
        - * Unlocks building Bletchley Park.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +12 City Security in every City.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Scientists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Satellites
        - Rationale: Maintain Satellites as our next research target. It unlocks the Apollo Program for a science-victory contingency and opens the path to The Internet and Lasers, which provide major strategic benefits regardless of final victory path. With Advanced Ballistics completing immediately, our atomic arsenal remains on track while we prepare for offensive operations against Songhai once our peace treaty expires.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Cult of Personality
        - Rationale: Keep Cult of Personality as our next adopted tenet. The +50% tourism modifier against civilizations fighting a common enemy will be valuable in the coming wars, and the free Great Person accelerates our military or scientific development. This Autocracy Level 3 tenet solidifies our ideological footing before major campaigns begin.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        - Flavors:
          - Offense: 85
          - Defense: 85
          - Mobilization: 40
          - CityDefense: 75
          - MilitaryTraining: 80
          - UseNuke: 95
          - Spaceship: 70
          - TileImprovement: 75
      - rationale: Reduce Mobilization to 40 because we are at military supply cap (131/130) and further raw unit production yields penalties; instead, prioritize unit upgrades and infrastructure. Trim Offense and Defense slightly to rebalance while keeping Air, Nuke, and Ranged at full priority to complete our atomic bomber fleet. Raise Spaceship to 70 since Satellites is next, unlocking Apollo Program as a backup victory path. Maintain high Diplomacy, Espionage, and Infrastructure to preserve city-state networks and economic growth during the final turns before hostilities resume with Songhai in 6 turns and Russia in 19.
  - tokens:
    - inputTokens: 152397
    - reasoningTokens: 16232
    - outputTokens: 365
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Looking at this turn, I need to assess the strategic situation and decide on macro-level adjustments.
            
            Current State Assessment:
            - I'm Spain (Isabella), leading in score (3893).
            - I'm pursuing a Domination victory (Conquest grand strategy).
            - I have multiple atomic bombs in production across cities (Seville, Cordoba, Toledo, Santiago, Kathmandu, Marrakech, Agaidika, Te-Moak, Moson Kahni).
            - I have peace with Songhai for 6 more turns (peace treaty expires soon).
            - I have a non-aggression pact with Russia for 19 more turns ("You promised not to declare war on each other for 19 more turns").
            - I'm publicly friendly with Russia (100) but privately hostile (-50).
            - Russia is building fighters and has military units near my borders.
            - My military strength is 33,918 vs Russia's 15,545 - I have a significant military advantage.
            - I have good relationships with Brazil, Netherlands, America, Korea (all 100 public).
            - World Congress voting in 6 turns. I have 16 delegates (39%), need 41 for diplomatic victory - not close.
            - Cultural victory: I'm influential with Korea, and approaching influential with several others (Brazil 20 turns, America 8 turns, Netherlands 14 turns, Russia 22 turns, Shoshone 49 turns).
            - Domination victory: I need 8 capitals. I have Madrid and Moson Kahni. Songhai has Gao and Seoul.
            
            Key considerations:
            1. Peace with Songhai expires in 6 turns. I've been hostile to Songhai (-100/-100) planning to eliminate their last city (Timbisha) after peace expires.
            2. I cannot attack Russia for 19 more turns due to the promise.
            3. Russia's opinion of me is very negative despite my public facade - they denounced me, think I'm warmongering, and find my military deployment threatening. They have Freedom while I have Autocracy.
            4. My economy is strong (10,408 gold, +244 GPT).
            5. I'm producing atomic bombs in many cities - these will be critical for the Russia war.
            6. Research: Advanced Ballistics completes in 1 turn. Next research was set to Satellites.
            
            Policy: Next policy is Cult of Personality in 14 turns.
            
            Diplomatic situation:
